===\n# Blueprint Analysis: drydock Layers Testing Uat Case\n\n## Commander Expectations\n\n- assert the completed ledger is a command-line cash ledger that runs as an executable named `ledger` at the application root\n- assert the ledger persists all data through the shipped persistence library over SQLite\n- assert a rejected command changes nothing\n- assert no command leaves an account balance below zero\n- assert every report is computed from stored database data, never embedded answers\n- assert the supplied end-to-end grader `sh sources/run.sh` passes with exit code zero\n\n## Crew\n\n| Crew | Charge |\n|---|---|\n| Commander | Defines intent and decides what done means. |\n| Team Lead | Confirms epic completeness and stakeholder expectations. |\n| Planning Crew | Authors atomic specifications and the ordered Manifest. |\n| Shipyard Crew | Builds the tickets without synchronous Commander access. |\n\n## Story List\n\nSummary: features: 6, stories: 17\n\n### Feature: Ledger Lifecycle\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| LEDGER-LIFECYCLE-001 | Initialize an empty ledger | `ledger init` prepares an empty database for use, writes nothing to stdout, and removes existing ledger records when rerun |\n| LEDGER-LIFECYCLE-002 | Count ledger rows per table | `ledger count` prints `table,rows` then accounts, categories, transactions in that order |\n\n### Feature: Bulk Load\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| BULK-LOAD-001 | Load accounts from CSV | `ledger load accounts <csv>` accepts a file whose header matches `account,name`, writes `table,loaded` and `accounts,<rows>`, and rejects the whole file with exit 3 on any invalid row |\n| BULK-LOAD-002 | Load categories from CSV | `ledger load categories <csv>` accepts a file whose header matches `category,description`, writes `table,loaded` and `categories,<rows>`, and rejects the whole file with exit 3 on any invalid row |\n| BULK-LOAD-003 | Load transactions from CSV | `ledger load transactions <csv>` accepts a file whose header matches the transaction columns, writes `table,loaded` and `transactions,<rows>`, and rejects the whole file with exit 3 when any row is invalid, references an unknown account or category, or would leave a balance negative |\n\n### Feature: Reporting\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| REPORTING-001 | Report account balances | `ledger report balances` prints `account,name,transactions,balance` per account ordered by code, then a `TOTAL` row |\n| REPORTING-002 | Report category totals | `ledger report categories` prints inflow, outflow, and net per category ordered by code, including empty categories, then a `TOTAL` row |\n| REPORTING-003 | Report monthly totals | `ledger report monthly` prints inflow, outflow, and net per `YYYY-MM` with transactions in ascending order, then a `TOTAL` row |\n\n### Feature: Account Management\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| ACCOUNT-001 | Add an account | `ledger account add <code> <name>` stores the account and prints its row; duplicate codes exit 3 |\n| ACCOUNT-002 | Get an account | `ledger account get <code>` prints the account row; an absent code exits 4 |\n| ACCOUNT-003 | Rename an account | `ledger account rename <code> <name>` updates the name and prints the row; an absent code exits 4 |\n| ACCOUNT-004 | Delete an account | `ledger account delete <code>` prints `deleted` and the code; an account with transactions is refused with exit 3; an absent code exits 4 |\n\n### Feature: Transaction Management\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| TRANSACTION-001 | Post a transaction | `ledger txn post` stores a row with the next `txn_id`, prints the row, and rejects invalid codes, amounts, dates, or negative balances with exit 3 |\n| TRANSACTION-002 | Get a transaction | `ledger txn get <id>` prints the transaction row; an absent id exits 4 |\n| TRANSACTION-003 | Amend a transaction amount | `ledger txn amend <id> <amount>` changes only the amount and prints the row; an absent id exits 4; a negative resulting balance exits 3 |\n| TRANSACTION-004 | Delete a transaction | `ledger txn delete <id>` removes the row and prints `deleted` and the id; an absent id exits 4; a negative resulting balance exits 3 |\n\n### Feature: Transfers\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| TRANSFER-001 | Transfer cash between accounts | `ledger transfer <from> <to> <amount> <date>` stores two rows in category `TRANSFER` with ids `n` and `n+1`, prints both, and stores both or neither |\n\n## Surfaced Acceptance Criteria\n\n| ID | Story ID | Criterion |\n|---|---|---|\n| AC-001 | LEDGER-LIFECYCLE-001 | `init` writes nothing to standard output and exits 0 |\n| AC-002 | BULK-LOAD-001 | A load that rejects any row stores none of the preceding rows |\n| AC-003 | BULK-LOAD-003 | A transaction load assigns each loaded `txn_id` from its file and prints the loaded count |\n| AC-004 | TRANSACTION-001 | A posted transaction receives one more than the largest stored `txn_id`, or `1` in an empty table |\n| AC-005 | TRANSFER-001 | A transfer with equal accounts, a non-positive amount, or an unknown account exits 3 and stores no rows |\n| AC-006 | ACCOUNT-004 | Deleting an account with no transactions succeeds and prints `deleted` and the code |\n\n## Relationship Model\n\n| Source or group | `sources/seed_transactions.csv` | Transactions reference account codes `DRAWER1`, `DRAWER2`, `PETTY`, `SAFE` | Accounts must load before transactions |\n| `sources/seed_categories.csv` | dependency | It records receipts and payments against accounts and produces balance, category, and monthly reports. It exists to give a shop an exact, always-consistent view of its cash, where every command either completes in full or changes nothing.\n\n## Constraints\n- Executable named `ledger` at the application root.\n- Python 3.11 or newer over SQLite, through the shipped Python persistence library.\n- All database access goes through the shipped persistence library and the generated relational layer; application code never opens a database driver directly.\n- Amounts stored as integer cents, never floating point.\n- `DATABASE_URL` selects the database; `DATABASE_PATH` is the fallback; `data/app.db` is the default.\n- Tests run under pytest; the application provides `bin/test.sh`, a POSIX-compatible runner.\n- Output is CSV with `\\n` line endings, no quoting, no trailing spaces.\n\n## Guardrails\n- A rejected command changes nothing; checks run before the first write.\n- No command leaves an account balance below zero.\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` and never embeds a report answer.\n- Never modify, rewrite, regenerate, or substitute `sources/run.sh` or the seed files.\n- Standard error is for failures only; standard output carries success results only.\n- Dates must be real calendar dates in `YYYY-MM-DD`.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT discovery-identity.json ===\n{\n  \"id\": \"discovery-identity\",\n  \"title\": \"Discovery: Project Identity\",\n  \"purpose\": \"Confirm the proposed display name and short description before planning.\",\n  \"questions\": [\n    {\n      \"id\": \"display_name\",\n      \"label\": \"Display Name\",\n      \"prompt\": \"The display name Drydock will use for this project. |\n|---|---|---|\n| REPORTING-001 | Report account balances | `ledger report balances` prints `account,name,transactions,balance` per account ordered by code, then a `TOTAL` row |\n| REPORTING-002 | Report category totals | `ledger report categories` prints inflow, outflow, and net per category ordered by code, including empty categories, then a `TOTAL` row |\n| REPORTING-003 | Report monthly totals | `ledger report monthly` prints inflow, outflow, and net per `YYYY-MM` with transactions in ascending order, then a `TOTAL` row |\n\n### Feature: Account Management\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| ACCOUNT-001 | Add an account | `ledger account add <code> <name>` stores the account and prints its row; duplicate codes exit 3 |\n| ACCOUNT-002 | Get an account | `ledger account get <code>` prints the account row; an absent code exits 4 |\n| ACCOUNT-003 | Rename an account | `ledger account rename <code> <name>` updates the name and prints the row; an absent code exits 4 |\n| ACCOUNT-004 | Delete an account | `ledger account delete <code>` prints `deleted` and the code; an account with transactions is refused with exit 3; an absent code exits 4 |\n\n### Feature: Transaction Management\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| TRANSACTION-001 | Post a transaction | `ledger txn post` stores a row with the next `txn_id`, prints the row, and rejects invalid codes, amounts, dates, or negative balances with exit 3 |\n| TRANSACTION-002 | Get a transaction | `ledger txn get <id>` prints the transaction row; an absent id exits 4 |\n| TRANSACTION-003 | Amend a transaction amount | `ledger txn amend <id> <amount>` changes only the amount and prints the row; an absent id exits 4; a negative resulting balance exits 3 |\n| TRANSACTION-004 | Delete a transaction | `ledger txn delete <id>` removes the row and prints `deleted` and the id; an absent id exits 4; a negative resulting balance exits 3 |\n\n### Feature: Transfers\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| TRANSFER-001 | Transfer cash between accounts | `ledger transfer <from> <to> <amount> <date>` stores two rows in category `TRANSFER` with ids `n` and `n+1`, prints both, and stores both or neither |\n\n## Surfaced Acceptance Criteria\n\n| ID | Story ID | Criterion |\n|---|---|---|\n| AC-001 | LEDGER-LIFECYCLE-001 | `init` writes nothing to standard output and exits 0 |\n| AC-002 | BULK-LOAD-001 | A load that rejects any row stores none of the preceding rows |\n| AC-003 | BULK-LOAD-003 | A transaction load assigns each loaded `txn_id` from its file and prints the loaded count |\n| AC-004 | TRANSACTION-001 | A posted transaction receives one more than the largest stored `txn_id`, or `1` in an empty table |\n| AC-005 | TRANSFER-001 | A transfer with equal accounts, a non-positive amount, or an unknown account exits 3 and stores no rows |\n| AC-006 | ACCOUNT-004 | Deleting an account with no transactions succeeds and prints `deleted` and the code |\n\n## Relationship Model\n\n| Source or group | Relationship type | Related source or group | Evidence | Delivery implication |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `sources/run.sh` | instruction-to-test | `sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md` | `run.sh` header and checks cite the command contract in `INSTRUCTIONS.md` | The grader is the terminal verification; stories must match its exact single-executable CLI named `ledger` at the application root. Inputs are CSV seed files and command-line arguments. Outputs are CSV on standard output for success and a single diagnostic line on standard error for failure. Persistence is SQLite through the shipped persistence library, with three tables: `accounts`, `categories`, and `transactions`. Amounts are integer cents.\n\nRequired execution flow: `init` → `load accounts` → `load categories` → `load transactions` → reports and mutations, with every mutation validated before its first write.\n\n### Story Realization Map\n\n- LEDGER-LIFECYCLE-001 — `sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md` Commands and Exit codes; `sources/run.sh` A1, Z1. Requires persistence capability and CSV parsing.\n- BULK-LOAD-002 — `sources/seed_categories.csv`; `sources/run.sh` B2. Requires persistence capability and CSV parsing.\n- BULK-LOAD-003 — `sources/seed_transactions.csv`, `sources/seed_transactions_bad.csv`; `sources/run.sh` B3, D1. seed files load exactly\n\nType: behavioral\nRequired: yes\nCriterion: When the supplied accounts, categories, and transactions files are loaded in order into an empty ledger, the application shall Reports match the known answers\n\nType: behavioral\nRequired: yes\nCriterion: The application shall compute account balances, category totals, and monthly totals from stored ledger data, matching the known answers in `sources/run.sh` byte for byte both before and after the supplied transaction and transfer workflows.\nVerification: proof\nPattern: ubiquitous\n\n## st-006: Accounts and transactions support create, read, update, and delete\n\nType: behavioral\nRequired: yes\nCriterion: The application shall retain account additions and renames and transaction postings and amendments across command invocations, return their current values on retrieval, remove successfully deleted records, refuse deletion of an account with transactions with exit code 3, and report an absent record with exit code 4.\nVerification: proof\nPattern: ubiquitous\n\n## st-007: A failed command changes nothing\n\nType: behavioral\nRequired: yes\nCriterion: If a load, account change, transaction change, or transfer violates a validation or business rule, then the application shall exit 3 and preserve all previously stored ledger records without adding, altering, or removing any record.\nVerification: proof\nPattern: unwanted\n\n## st-008: No balance goes negative\n\nType: behavioral\nRequired: yes\nCriterion: If loading transactions, posting, amending, deleting, or transferring would leave any account balance below zero, then the application shall reject the entire command with exit code 3 and preserve the prior balances.\nVerification: proof\nPattern: unwanted\n\n## st-010: Transfers conserve cash\n\nType: behavioral\nRequired: yes\nCriterion: When a valid transfer is requested between distinct accounts, the application shall record an equal debit and credit in category `TRANSFER`, return both transaction rows, and update both account balances without changing the total cash held.\nVerification: proof\nPattern: event\n\n## st-011: Command results follow the CLI contract\n\nType: behavioral\nRequired: yes\nCriterion: The application shall return the CSV headers, row ordering, two-decimal monetary values, and exit codes specified in `sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md`, returning exit 2 for unknown commands or reports and incorrect argument counts, and writing failures only to standard error.\nVerification: proof\nPattern: ubiquitous\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n=== It records receipts and payments against accounts and produces balance, category, and monthly reports. It exists to give a shop an exact, always-consistent view of its cash, where every command either completes in full or changes nothing.\n\n## Constraints\n- Executable named `ledger` at the application root.\n- Python 3.11 or newer over SQLite, through the shipped Python persistence library.\n- All database access goes through the shipped persistence library and the generated relational layer; application code never opens a database driver directly.\n- Amounts stored as integer cents, never floating point.\n- `DATABASE_URL` selects the database; `DATABASE_PATH` is the fallback; `data/app.db` is the default.\n- Tests run under pytest; the application provides `bin/test.sh`, a POSIX-compatible runner.\n- Output is CSV with `\\n` line endings, no quoting, no trailing spaces.\n\n## Guardrails\n- A rejected command changes nothing; checks run before the first write.\n- No command leaves an account balance below zero.\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` and never embeds a report answer.\n- Never modify, rewrite, regenerate, or substitute `sources/run.sh` or the seed files.\n- Standard error is for failures only; standard output carries success results only.\n- Dates must be real calendar dates in `YYYY-MM-DD`.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT discovery-identity.json ===\n{\n  \"id\": \"discovery-identity\",\n  \"title\": \"Discovery: Project Identity\",\n  \"purpose\": \"Confirm the proposed display name and short description before planning.\",\n  \"questions\": [\n    {\n      \"id\": \"display_name\",\n      \"label\": \"Display Name\",\n      \"prompt\": \"The display name Drydock will use for this project.
